## Document 015: 115年9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 (2026-10-13)

下次發布日期：115 年 11 月 11 日下午 4 時

電   話：02-2322-7555 
115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳、暫繳稅款俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品等進口代徵稅額增加，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額 534 億 元 ， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

4
三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 9 月中央政府賦稅收入(不含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵淨額
2 兆 3,603 億元(占分配預算數 119.3%)，較上年同期增加 2,553 億元
(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 9 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款)實徵
淨額 1 兆 898 億元(占分配預
算數 109.4%)，較上年同期增
加 4,527 億元(+71.0%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配之營業稅
增加 4,030 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 9 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 195 億元，較
上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

儒鴻(1476 TT) Trading Buy

營運穩定成長，長期展望正向。 
投資建議：預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，
稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027 年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂
單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，YoY+3.84%，稅 後 EPS 25.52
元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，預期未來幾個季
度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新提高
生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投
產，推動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為
Trading Buy，目標價 385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。  
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元：儒鴻 3Q26 營收
維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%， QoQ+4.82%，毛利率亦
受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因
素，順利提升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。預期
4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻
營收維持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，
YoY+14.34%，QoQ+0.20%，毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維
持六個月左右：美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期
極端的關稅政策不會再發生，不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目
前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，預期未來幾個月的
訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能見度維
持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因
應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產：儒鴻持續擴增新產能及
自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南二地的生
產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目
前印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來
的成長空間仍大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下
一段成長的動能。

### Page 2

個股報告 
2026 年 11 月 11 日

2 群益投顧 
  儒鴻的 ESG：  
儒鴻 2016 年即制定溫室氣體盤查管理程序，並依循「儒鴻企業環安衛及能
源政策」設置溫室氣體盤查推行委員會，由董事長室擔任主任委員，推動廠
區溫室氣體盤查，並以 2017 年作為公司溫室氣體盤查的基準年，推動溫室
氣體減量。此外，致力於用水回收，除了在製程蒸氣冷凝回收使用外，更引
進新技術改善廢水回收。 
公司簡介： 
儒鴻為台灣主要紡織業者，具染整、織布、成衣上下游一貫生產能力，主要
產品為各式成衣，並生產機能布料應用在各類運動休閒服飾上。國際各知名
服飾品牌及通路商如 NIKE、 UA、 LULULEMON 、 TARGET、 COSTCO 皆
為儒鴻的客戶。 
預估 4Q26 營收維持成長，稅後 EPS 6.09 元： 
儒鴻 3Q26 營收維持高檔為 105.72 億元，YoY+9.74%，QoQ+4.82%，毛利率
亦受惠客戶新產品出貨增加，產品組合優化及匯率相對友善等因素，順利提
升至 31.18%，稅後 EPS 為 6.36 元，符合預期。 
預期 4Q26 由於成衣價格上漲且客戶新品訂單持續出貨，可推動儒鴻營收維
持成長，預估儒鴻 4Q26 營收為 105.93 億元，YoY+14.34%， QoQ+0.20%，
毛利率 31.23%，稅後 EPS 6.09 元。 
美國期中選舉結束，市場不確定因素消除，儒鴻訂單能見度維持六個月左
右： 
美國期中選舉在民主黨獲勝的結果下，可預期極端的關稅政策不會再發生，
不確定性因素逐漸消除，不過目前也沒看到品牌端有追加訂單的動作出現，
預期未來幾個月的訂單及終端銷售會處於正常的水準。目前儒鴻的訂單能
見度維持在六個月左右，市場庫存也在相對健康水準，整體市況穩定。 
儒鴻持續投入自動化設備更新提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，
印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產： 
儒鴻持續擴增新產能及自動化設備更新以因應客戶需求，除了在台灣、越南
二地的生產設備持續更新外，印尼廠新產能也計劃在 4Q27 產能投產。目前
印尼廠的產能利用率維持滿載水準，印尼廠的生產需求未來的成長空間仍
大，預期等印尼廠三期投產後，可為儒鴻帶來下一段成長的動能。 
投資建議： 
預估儒鴻 2026 年營收為 408.71 億元，YoY+7.59%，稅後 EPS 24.79 元。2027
年預期在產能稼動維持高檔及客戶訂單穩定下，營收成長至 424.43 億元，
YoY+3.84%，稅後 EPS 25.52 元。考量：(1)3Q26 毛利率如預期的順利回升，
預期未來幾個季度的毛利率可維持現有水準。(2)持續投入自動化設備更新
提高生產效率，並擴建新產能因應客戶需求，印尼新廠預計 4Q27 投產，推
動長線的成長動能。綜上所述，對儒鴻的投資建議為 Trading Buy，目標價
385 元 (2027 年 PER 15.5X)。

6 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】 
  評等 定義 
  強力買進(Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間≥ 35%  
  買進(Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<35%  
  區間操作(Trading Buy) 5%≤首次評等潛在上漲空間<15%

中立(Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等 
預期近期股價將處於盤整 
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】 
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。 
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。 
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」、「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。 
停止推薦情境： 
1. 達目標價。 
2. 雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。 
3. 推薦後股價不漲反跌，亦將出具降評報告。

【免責聲明】 
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

研 究 報 告                
中砂(1560.TT) 
產業：電機機械 
上修資本支出用於擴產隱含展望正向，看好毛利率延續成⻑趨勢 
 投資評價與建議 
3Q26毛利率表現優於預期，且公司上修資本支出用於擴產，隱含主要業務展
望正向，加上公司積極優化產品結構隱含明年營收、獲利均具上修空間；我們
給予40倍PE，維持對公司業務之正向看法，並看好毛利率維持成⻑動能。 
 重點評論分析 
4Q26三大業務營收、獲利持續改善：觀察ABU業務中，預期高階砂輪與ABF鑽
⽯機密模具需求持續成⻑，DBU業務出貨動能則維持強勁，加上公司規劃針對
SBU部分品項漲價約10%，將帶動營收、毛利率改善。我們預估本季營收有望
季增5.5%至28.4億元，毛利率44.5%，稅後EPS 4.37元。 
上修今年資本支出預算用於擴產與新品開發，隱含營運前景正向：基於DBU、
SBU客戶需求強勁，及公司持續優化產品組合，公司上修今年資本支出預算至
22.6億元(原16~17億元)，除了先前已規畫SBU 3Q27月產能至43萬片(已宣
布)，本次則上修今明年底鑽⽯碟月產能分別至8、11萬顆(原7萬顆以上/9.5萬
顆)，此外將購買土地廠房用於開發先進封裝用之減薄鑽⽯砂輪新品，以及後續
鑽⽯碟擴產需求。而在2027年展望方面，我們仍維持2027年三大業務均正向
成⻑之看法，其中DBU、SBU成⻑動能最為強勁，另ABU業務中，公司布局之
先進封裝用之減薄砂輪有望於2H27貢獻營收。預估2027年營收年增25.7%至
129.7億元，毛利率46%，稅後EPS 21.7元。 
 投資風險 
DBU擴產進度不如預期。

### Page 2

2

| 營運概況 
3Q26毛利率表現優於預期：3Q26營收季增8.1%至26.9億元，毛利率42.2%，稅後EPS 3.88元，其中毛利率受惠
於：(1)台灣工具機客戶需求回溫、ABF鑽⽯機密模具用量增加，帶動ABU高階產品比重提升，挹注該業務毛利率
由2Q26的超過25%提升至3Q26的35%；(2)針對DBU大客戶之2/1.6奈米、先進封裝鑽⽯碟用量持續增加，使營收
規模擴大，我們推估毛利率由2Q26的50~55%提升至55%以上。 
4Q26三大業務營收、獲利持續改善：觀察：(1)ABU業務中，預期高階砂輪與ABF鑽⽯機密模具需求持續成⻑，至
於泰國、日本子公司出貨動能則持平看待，預估營收微幅季增，毛利率則有望持續向上；(2)DBU業務中，出貨動
能則維持強勁，因台系大客戶先進製程、封裝需求維持強勁，另美系記憶體、IDM客戶需求呈現逐季成⻑，加上
成熟製程廠商產能利用率持續提升，預估營收仍有雙位數季增；(3)SBU業務中，12吋月產能維持40萬片滿載，但
公司計畫針對部分品項價格調漲約10%，加上持續優化產品組合，將帶動營收、毛利率改善。預估本季營收有望
季增5.5%至28.4億元，毛利率44.5%，稅後EPS 4.37元。 
上修今年資本支出預算用於擴產與新品開發，隱含營運前景正向：基於DBU、SBU客戶需求強勁，及公司持續優
化產品組合，公司上修今年資本支出預算至22.6億元(原16~17億元)，除了先前已規畫SBU 3Q27月產能至43萬片
(已宣布)，本次則上修今明年底鑽⽯碟月產能分別至8、11萬顆(原7萬顆以上/9.5萬顆)，此外將購買土地廠房用於
開發先進封裝用之減薄鑽⽯砂輪新品，以及後續鑽⽯碟擴產需求。而在2027年展望方面，我們仍維持2027年三大
業務均正向成⻑之看法，動能包含：(1)預估DBU主要客戶A16/2、3奈米明年底月產能將由今年底的9~10、15~17
分別提升至14~16、19~20萬片，尤其先進製程CMP道數、規格相較於成熟製程提升，將帶動鑽⽯碟用量、平均
單價增加，另美系IDM合作順利(目前公司於18A市占約50%，公司持續推進14A之合作)，加上成熟製程、記憶體
客戶擴產挹注；(2)SBU除了有明年額外3萬片之產能挹注，公司針對現有產能也將持續透過產品組合優化等方式改
善獲利，且看好2H27美系記憶體特殊晶圓用量將比1H27成⻑；(3)ABU方面，則基於台灣ABF廠商持續擴產，公
司布局之新品先進封裝減薄砂輪有望在2H27於新廠貢獻營收，加上公司將中低毛利率產品搬至泰國、日本子公司
生產以此優化成本結構。我們預估2027年營收年增25.7%至129.7億元，毛利率46%，稅後EPS 21.7元。

## Document 068: 2026年10月通胀数据点评：PPI上涨5.7%，持续性几何？

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月通胀数据点评：PPI上涨5.7%，持续性几何？.pdf
Report source: 金融街证券
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 1

### Page 1

本报告版权仅为本公司所有，未经书面许可，任何机构和个人不得以任何形式将研报内容和相关信息对外公布、转发、转载、传播、复制、编辑、修改、引用等。如有上述违法行为，本公司保留追究相关方法律责任的权利。
市场有风险，投资需谨慎。投资者不应将本报告作为作出投资决策的唯一参考因素，亦不应认为本报告可以取代自己的判断。在决定投资前，如有需要，投资者务必向专业人士咨询并谨慎决策。
（一）本轮PPI修复成色
2026年10月，PPI同比上涨5.7%，总量回升背后行业价格修复仍明显分化。以2025年7月为观察起点，
40个工业大类行业中，22个行业价格上涨，占比55%，上涨覆盖略超过半数；其余18个行业价格继续下降，
主要包括汽车、通用设备、专用设备，以及食品、服装、医药等中下游行业。
价格回升的幅度与历史跌幅的收复程度也存在差异，目前仍有30个行业的价格指数低于2022年10月水
平，占比75%。部分行业虽然已经反弹，但尚不足以弥补此前累积的跌幅，多数行业价格仍处于相对低位。

风险提示：国际大宗商品价格大幅波动；终端需求恢复不及预期；上游涨价向中下游传导不畅；行业权重及黄金贡献估算
存在偏差，实际价格走势偏离翘尾测算假设。

（三）CPI修复进入平台期
10月CPI和核心CPI同比分别上涨0.7%和0.8%，均较9月回落0.2个百分点。核心CPI涨幅回落约六成来
自黄金支撑减弱；剔除黄金后的超核心价格指标同比约为0.84%，同样处于低增速平台。

回顾本轮修复，2025年下半年至2026年初，剔除食品、能源和黄金后的价格指标明显回升，表明价
格改善并非完全依赖特殊项目；但2026年春季以来，该指标大致趋稳，终端价格修复进入平台期，进一
步上行动力仍显不足。同期，PPI同比滚动均值持续上升，工业品价格走强尚未伴随终端基础消费价格的
同步加速，二者的分化尚无收敛迹象。但基础消费价格保持相对稳定，也暂未显示终端需求压力持续加
深。
2027年月份 PPI同比翘尾

（二）涨价动能与正翘尾支撑短期PPI
短期内，PPI同比仍有两方面支撑：一是当期涨价动能尚未消退，10月PPI环比上涨0.7%，显示工业品
价格仍在上行；二是年内已经发生的价格上涨，将通过正翘尾对2027年同比读数形成支撑。
假设2026年11-12月价格维持10月水平，按现有口径测算，2027年1月PPI翘尾因素可拉动同比上涨近5

个百分点。正翘尾对2027年PPI同比的较强支撑可延续至一季度，4月明显减弱，7-8月阶段性回升，9月大
幅消退。这一假设用于衡量截至10月的既有涨价的滞后影响。考虑到当前价格上涨动能仍在，将年末价格
维持在10月水平作为现阶段的基准情景，具有一定合理性。
从结构看，按底稿行业权重近似测算，在2026年末价格维持当前水平的假设下，2027年1月，石油、
煤炭及其他燃料加工业,化学原料及化学制品制造业,计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业,煤炭开采和洗
选业的翘尾贡献分别约为1.37、1.08、0.63和0.62个百分点。四个行业权重合计约24.7%，贡献合计约
3.70个百分点，贡献当期PPI同比翘尾的74.2%。因此，短期PPI同比读数有较强支撑，但价格修复基础尚
待巩固。悲观情形，若能源化工价格回落的同时，中下游价格未能接续修复，并伴随降价范围扩大和订单
走弱，则即使PPI同比处于高位，也需关注价格下行压力重新累积。

2344 華邦電(Winbond)

報告日期：2026/11/06 研 究 員：王蕎程 (Joe Wang) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要 
 3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 為 8.06 元。 受惠記憶體
報價上漲與產品組合改善，第三季毛利率達 70.57%，預估第
四季營收 839.78 億元，QoQ +2.0%，毛利率 70.77%。 
 擴產與 16 奈米轉進帶動 2027 年位元出貨成長。 高雄廠
Module A 新增產能及台中廠 Flash 擴產陸續貢獻，搭配
DDR4、LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 需求，支持營運成長。 
 預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 23.29／37.45 元。 AI 伺服器與
企業級 SSD 需求為主要動能，CUBE 及矽電容拓展新應用。

一、3Q26 稅後 EPS 7.58 元，預估 4Q26 營收持續季增 
華邦電為利基型記憶體廠，產品涵蓋客製化記憶體、NOR Flash 及
SLC NAND。3Q26 營收 823.31 億元，QoQ +37.6%、YoY +278.2%；
營業毛利 581.01 億元，毛利率 70.57%；營業利益 437.84 億元，營
業利益率 53.18%；稅後純益 354.22 億元， EPS 7.58 元。營收結構
以客製化記憶體占 58%、Flash 占 32%、邏輯 IC 占 9%。其中，客
製化記憶體平均售價季增近 60%，Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，為
第三季成長主因。展望第四季，公司表示 AI 需求維持強勁，部分
PC 與消費性客戶調整拉貨，整體營運仍將成長，惟增幅低於前幾
季。考量合約價格續升及產品組合改善，預估 4Q26 營收 839.78 億
元，QoQ +2.0%；毛利率 70.77%，營業利益 448.28 億元，稅後 EPS 
8.06 元。

二、高雄廠擴產，支應 DDR4 及 LPDDR4 長期需求 
公司持續供應 DDR3、DDR4 及 LPDDR4，受惠主要記憶體原廠調
整產品配置，以及車用、工業客戶產品生命週期長、替換供應來
源不易，長期供貨需求具支撐。高雄廠 Module A 月產能預計由約
1.5 萬片提高至 2.4 萬至 2.5 萬片，新增產能於 2027 年陸續貢獻，
並提高 16 奈米製程比重，同時增加晶圓投入與單片晶圓位元產
出。法人看好擴產搭配製程轉進可帶動 2027 年 DRAM 位元出貨顯
著成長，中信預估增幅超過 100%。本報告採新增產能與製程效益
逐步反映的假設，預估 2027 年營收逐季成長。長期方面，董事會
通過 731 億元資本支出，用於高雄廠 Module B 土建、機電及長交
期設備，規劃月產能約 5 萬至 6 萬片，預計 2029 年下半年開始小
量出貨，並以長期供貨協議支應策略客戶需求。

三、AI 帶動 CUBE 與矽電容拓展新應用 
AI 伺服器機櫃內 GPU、BMC、SmartNIC 及交換器等元件均需 Flash
儲存韌體與設定資料，隨運算及管理架構複雜化，帶動使用顆數
與容量提升。公司預估供應 AI 生態系的 Flash 位元出貨量於 2026
年成長 112%，2027 年再增 67%，其中高容量 NOR Flash 為主要動
能；企業級 SSD 亦增加 DRAM 需求，擴大公司在伺服器供應鏈的
參與。法人看好台中廠擴產與高容量產品組合改善。新產品方面，
CUBE 主要布局 AI 加速器與推論應用，預計 2027 年下半年增加出
貨；矽電容已小量出貨，既有產能多獲客戶預訂，並透過代工模
式切入 AI 晶片、先進封裝及高速光模組應用，逐步增加產品附加
價值與長期營收來源。 
四. 投資建議：買進，目標價 180 元 
華邦電為全球最大 NOR Flash 廠亦是利基型 DRAM 廠，AI 帶動各
式記憶體需求增加，產能仍供不應求下，獲利將逐季成長。公司
針對邊緣 AI 發展之 CUBE 解決方案將於 2027 年開始貢獻，矽電容
亦為新的業務機會，中長期而言 AI、HPC、IoT、自駕車等新產品
新應用將帶動更多記憶體需求。考量到 1) AI 帶動之記憶體供不應
求將延續至 2028 年、2)華邦電今明年營收獲利創高、3)CUBE、矽
電容具成長潛力，參考 2027 年 EPS 給予 5 倍本益比，目標價 180
元，建議買進。

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況

一.公司簡介 
華邦電子於 1987 年九月由華新麗華集團轉投資成立，為自有品牌
IC 設計及製造廠商，並為全世界第五大 DRAM 供應商，且為國內
少數擁有晶圓廠之積體電路設計公司，同時也是全球利基型記憶
體的主要供應商。2008 年公司將邏輯 IC 事業分割成立新唐科技，
新唐產品線以消費性電子 IC、電腦 IC 兩大產品線為主，並同時擁
有一座 6 吋晶圓廠。華邦電在 NOR Flash 為全球最大供應商，產
品上從中低容量市場拓展至中高容量市場。NOR Flash 主要同業為
旺宏、兆易創新、Infineon。

二.2026 年營運概況及展望

1. 2026 下半年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 2026 年 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 下半年，預估記憶體求維持強勁下，價格持續上漲，唯
因價格基期已高，加上傳統消費性產品追價力道有限，漲幅不若
上半年，不過預估今年隨著價格上漲，加上製程轉換帶動位元出

3 
貨量增加，及新產能貢獻，營收逐季成長，毛利率持續提升，預
估華邦電 2026 年營收 2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，
稅後淨利 1,047.92 億元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

3. 2027 年記憶體更加緊缺，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 帶動之記憶體缺貨狀況延續，加上全球新增產能
有限，緊缺情況更加嚴峻，而儘管終端電子產品需求不一，不過
未來 AI 落地後每單位產品之記憶體搭載量持續成長，使得記憶體
位元出貨提升，2027 年公司營運仍正向。DDR4 在全球三大廠逐步
退出下價格自 2Q25 起漲，且因終端電子產品仍有一定需求，價格
將維持高檔，NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 亦因供給端增產有限，價格
逐步回升，其中 SLC Nand 因大廠退出，華邦將成為全球最大供應
商，而應用於 AI Server 的高容量 NOR Flash 持續供不應求，預期
將推升價格上行至 2027 年。華邦電製程技術方面，20 奈米 DRAM
產品仍為主要出貨主力，應用於 DDR3、DDR4、LPDDR 產品，16
奈米製程已開發完成，2027 年將有一定比例轉換至 16 奈米；NOR 
Flash 營收穩定成長，NAND Flash 24 奈米及 32 奈米 SLC Nand 今年
2Q 貢獻顯著提升。因應記憶體需求強勁，公司 Flash 與 DRAM 擴
產計畫進行中，華邦電 2025 年資本支出為台幣 55 億元，2026 年
將提升至 395 億元，其中生產設備資本支出約占 95%，大部分用
於高雄產能及製程提升，少部分用於台中廠製程提升，高雄廠產
能將由現在 15k 擴充至年底的 24k，屆時位元出貨量可望翻倍，同
時啟動高雄 Module B 投資計劃，規劃月產能可達 50k~60k，將分
階段擴充，預估 2027 年 1 月動工，2029 年 1 月裝機，2029 年底量
產。2027 年預估 DRAM、NOR Flash 及 SLC NAND 價格持續上漲帶
動營收獲利成長，預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛
利率 72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

4. CUBE 及矽電容為新成長動能 
AI 時代來臨，華邦電長期專注於利基型 DRAM，針對 AI 將主攻邊
緣運算市場，邊緣運算之記憶體容量不需像 AI Server 那麼高，為
華邦電可著墨之區塊，因此華邦電提供 CUBE(Customized Ultra 
Bandwidth Element 客製化超高頻寬記憶體元件)解決方案，CUBE
具備超低功耗和超高性能、小尺寸、高成本效益，從而大幅提升
系統能力、回應時間和能源效率，以滿足邊緣運算平臺上快速增
長的 AI 應用需求。CUBE 優點在於 1)SoC 推疊在上面，散熱效果
較好、2) 可在 CUBE Die 上做穿孔(TSV)，降低成本、3) 3D 推疊
適合較小的終端裝置。目前華邦電與多家客戶開發 CUBE 方案，
預估 2026 年底開始貢獻，2027 年放量。另外因應 AI 晶片功耗劇
增、瞬間電流波動，以及先進封裝的空間限制，矽電容具有極低
的等效串聯電感與寄生電感，能在極短時間內補償電流，維持電

叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估

一. 3Q26 毛利率升至 70.57%，EPS 7.58 元 
華邦電 3Q26 營收 823.31 億元(QoQ+37.6%、YoY+278.2%)，其中記
憶體事業營收 742.84 億元(QoQ+43.7%)。受惠客製化記憶體平均售
價季增近 60%、Flash 平均售價季增約 30%，合併毛利率提升至
70.57%，營業利益 437.84 億元，歸屬母公司稅後淨利 354.22 億元，
EPS 7.58 元。展望第四季，記憶體合約價格持續上升，惟部分 PC
與消費性客戶調整拉貨，預估營收 839.78 億元(QoQ+2.0%)，毛利
率 70.77%，EPS 8.06 元。

二. 2026 年記憶體維持上漲格局，預估 EPS 23.29 元 
展望 2026 年下半年，AI 伺服器需求強勁，帶動 DDR4、LPDDR4
及高容量 Flash 價格持續上漲，惟第四季部分 PC 與消費性客戶調
整拉貨，營運成長幅度較第三季收斂。預估華邦電 2026 年營收
2,644.05 億元(YoY+195.7%)，毛利率 67.17%，稅後淨利 1,047.92 億
元(YoY+2,545.0%)，EPS 23.29 元。

三. 2027 年擴產與製程轉進帶動成長，預估 EPS 37.45 元 
展望 2027 年，AI 伺服器與企業級 SSD 需求持續成長，DDR4、
LPDDR4 及高容量 NOR Flash 供需維持緊俏，加上高雄廠新增產能
與 16 奈米製程轉進，帶動位元出貨增加，CUBE 及矽電容亦逐步
貢獻營收。預估 2027 年營收 3,833.59 億元(YoY+45.0%)，毛利率
72.29%，稅後淨利 1,685.15 億元(YoY+60.8%)，EPS 37.45 元。

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對義隆電的投資建議維持中立，係因(1)受到產業影響，3Q26
財報大幅不如預期，(2)4Q26 淡季更淡，預估下修幅度不小，(3)27
年產業狀況仍受到 CPU、記憶體等元件影響，目前較難樂觀看待，
(4)下修 26、27 年獲利預估。綜合以上，下修義隆電 26 年 EPS 預
估為 6.26 元(原估 9.37 元，-22%YOY)，及預估 27 年 EPS 為 6.58
元(原估 10.14 元，+5.1%YOY)，因為 NB 市場除了先前的記憶體影
響外，目前更受到 CPU 缺貨的影響，短線上義隆電的營運動能受到
連帶影響大，靜待無人機相關 IC 營收在 2H27 放大，故投資建議維
持中立，目標價 132 元。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅滑落，獲利不如預期： 
義隆電 3Q26 營收僅為 22.2 億元(原估 30.85 億元)，衰退
30.7%QOQ，衰退 33%YOY，營收大規模衰退，除了記憶體漲
價外，因為 Agent AI 興起，使得 CPU 缺貨嚴重，另外因為
成本反映至終端產品，故使得需求更是急凍。毛利率僅
44.7%，相對於 2Q26 下滑 2.7 個百分點，主要就是各類成
本漲價，包含晶圓代工、封裝及 TOUCH PAD 內的 PCB 及被
動元件等，另外產品組合也轉差所致。另外業外又有高達
1 億元的金融評價損失，故使得稅後淨利僅為 1.68 億元
(原估 6.23 億元)，衰退 80.9%QOQ，衰退 79.1%YOY，EPS 
0.55 元(原估 EPS 2.05 元)。3Q26 產品組合為：AI 14%，
觸控 73%，非觸控 13%，其中 TOUCH PAD 43%，TOUCH SCREEN 
14%，指紋 16%，POINT STICK 19%，MCU+SENSOR 4%。

2. 3Q26 未給展望，但受到 CPU 缺貨影響嚴重： 
義隆電因為最近四季合併（個別）財務報表營業外收支淨
額合計數占稅前損益合計數之絕對值若大於 10%，在法說
會中不能提及預測性資訊，但在法說會有提到 4Q26 本來
就是季節性淡季，另外客戶在上半年非常積極拉貨，下半
年開始調整庫存，故 4Q26 推估各產線仍有下滑，僅有 AI
相關產品會成長。故研究部下修 4Q26 營收預估為 16.5 億
元(原估 31.7 億元)，衰退 25.7%QOQ，衰退 42.3%YOY，稅
後淨利同步下修預估為 1.51 億元(原估 6.38 億元)，衰退
10.1%QOQ，衰退 79.5%YOY，EPS 0.5 元(原估 EPS 2.1 元)。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

3. 對 27 年 NB 產業看法仍未定，但義隆仍有新商機： 
義隆電在法說會表示，NB 產業有各研調機構都有看法，目
前普遍較為保守，但未來仍端視 27 年記憶體、各元件及
CPU 的狀況，非單一供應商決定市況，故認為在此階段仍
不易下結論。但義隆電在 NB 觸控相關 IC 方面，因為美系
競爭對手被併購後，義隆電有 SHARE GAIN 部分市場，另外
也提供更廣泛的產品選項供客戶選擇，故公司認為本身的
NB 相關 IC 出貨量 27 年將優於市場表現。

4. 無人機貢獻性確實提升，大貢獻性將落在 2H27： 
1H26 無人機營收占比約低個位數，3Q26 已經達到 14%，
4Q26 有望在持續攀升。目前主要配合的客戶為國內廠商，
該國內廠商前天六項標案全數得標，而義隆電現在主要出
貨的產品為 CAMERA 模組，目前為兩個鏡頭一個模組+AI 
BOX，單價為數十美元，目前送樣 4 鏡頭+避障+AI BOX 的
解決方案，單價高達 800 美元，有望在 1H27 逐步開始量
產，下半年完整貢獻。推估 AI 產品營收在 27 年成長約
30%YOY 以上。

5. 大幅下修26年預估，27年預估也較為保守：  
3Q26受到客戶已經提前拉貨影響，加上相關零組件及封裝
成本提升，使得3Q26營收及獲利大幅不如預期，加上4Q26
又有下修，故研究部下修26年義隆電營收預估為103.02億
元(原估126.8億元)，EPS下修預估至6.26元(原估EPS 9.37
元)。展望27年，目前研究部以27年NB整體出貨量高雙位數
衰退，但義隆電整體為低雙位數衰退，雖然義隆電無人機
相關產品推進至整合型產品，單價大幅提升下，仍下修27
年營收預估為112.3億元(原估140億元)，成長9%YOY，稅後
淨利下修預估為20億元(原估30.8億元)，成長5.1%YOY，
EPS 6.58元(原估10.14元)。

In-line 3Q26 results
Co-Tech reported 3Q26 results:
• 3Q revenue was NT$3,169mn (+13% q/q, +60% y/y).
• GM was 26.7% (-70bps q/q, +620bps y/y), 20/150bps below MSe/Street,
which we attributed to higher copper pricing and electricity rates (link).
• OP came in at NT$757mn (+11% q/q, +125% y/y), 1% above MSe but 5% below
the Street, for OpM of 23.9% (-50bps q/q, +690bps y/y), 30bps above MSe
but 140bps below the Street.

• As a result, NP was NT$608mn (+12% q/q, +140% y/y), in line with MSe but
4% below the Street, for EPS of NT$2.41.
• Co-Tech’s board also approved up to NT$7bn of CBs to fund the company's
Malaysian expansion. Issue price will be set by auction. Conversion price is
TBD pending regulatory approval.
• Reiterate OW; PT NT$745.

Sep 2026)

### Page 3

M
Update
Morgan Stanley Research 3
Valuation Methodology and Risks
Co-Tech Development Corp. (8358.TWO)
Base case, derived from a residual income (RI) model. Key assumptions include a cost of equity
of 9.3% (beta of 1.0, equity premium of 8.7% and risk-free rate of 1%), medium-term growth
rate of 11.5%, and terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger AI demand
n Slower-than-expected capacity expansion by competitors
n Faster-than-expected material upgrades

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 147: 261110_ubs_allring

### Page 1

ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
All Ring Tech
Bright 2027 outlook with multiple drivers
Intact 2027 outlook
All Ring's 2027 outlook is robust, supported by three key drivers. 1) We expect TSMC
and ASE to continue accelerating CoWoS capacity expansion, with industry capacity
reaching ~270kwpm by end-2027E, up from ~160kwpm by end-2026. We estimate
TSMC and ASE’s capacity could reach ~180kwpm and ~70kwpm, respectively, by
2027E. 2) Second, with Intel's EMIB-T progressing on track toward volume production

by early 2028, we expect related equipment demand to begin ramping in 2027,
providing a new growth driver for All Ring. 3) CPO’s fiber array unit (FAU) coupling tools
start contributing to All Ring's 2027 revenue, with demand from Nvidia and AMD.
More concrete CPO development in 2027-28
Our industry feedback suggests Nvidia is taking the lead to aggressively push CPO mass
production in 2027 for scale up switch solution. AMD is also engaging with key

equipment suppliers, in preparation for competing CPO solution. Competition among
the equipment vendors is fierce, but we believe All Ring is well positioned in FAU
coupling as a tier 1 supplier, given the superior execution in driving higher tool
throughput versus peers. Considering the meaningful tool demand in 2027-28, we
forecast All Ring to recognize ~100 tools for revenue in 2027E, contributing ~12% of All
Ring's sales next year. Potential upside to our current estimates could come from larger

TAM for faster capacity expansion, All Ring securing higher share / ASP through better
tool performance.
Lower Q3 gross margin due to mix
Q3 EPS came in below LSEG consensus by -10% as gross margin of 48.5% missed
consensus of 54.2%. Lower gross margin could be due to a higher mix of automation
products, which carries a lower margin vs corporate average. For Q4, considering lighter
seasonality and current visibility, we model sales to decline by -28% from Q3 with gross

margin of 53% and EPS of NT$7.57.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and target of $1700
Post Q3 results, we fine-tune 2026 EPS by -4% due to lower gross margin in the quarter,
though our estimates for 2027/28 are largely kept unchanged. Our 2027E/2028E EPS
are 3%/30% ahead of Consensus pre-earnings. We maintain our Buy rating and price
target of $1700 based on 27x average 2027-28E PE.

Company Description
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based supplier of automation equipment for the
semiconductor back-end packaging and passive component manufacturing industries. The
company's products include underfill dispensers, heatsink placement and automated optical
inspection (AOI) tools for advanced packaging equipment, particularly for heterogeneous

integration. Revenue is generated primarily from sales of semiconductor and electronic
manufacturing equipment and related automation solutions, with more than 90% of
revenue derived from Taiwan.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value All Ring based on PE methodology.
Risks for All Ring and the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in

the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech

companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in All Ring
gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in All
Ring losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could

lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to All
Ring, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact All Ring’s
margins for equipment shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do
About It: As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably
above $6 per gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major
theme of 2026. Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event,

on track to be one of the strongest on record. The upshot: more extreme weather
from droughts to heavy rains, warmer temperatures globally, lower crop production in
commodity-exporting economies, and higher prices across major agricultural products.
Importantly, these pressures won’t be equally distributed and will largely depend on the
magnitude and timing of weather events. Among the most vulnerable will be heavily
import-dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa and parts of Asia, while developed

economies that count food as a smaller share of consumer spending should be better
insulated. For investors, the prospect of a very strong El Niño only further reinforces our
conviction in several structural trends including a return to higher inflation and higher
interest rates, elevated commodity prices amid heightened geopolitical risk and supply
chain shortages, and greater investment in energy infrastructure and supply chain
resilience. In portfolios, we favor commodities as both an inflation hedge and diversifier.

Market View—Another Tale of Market Rotation: October offered another reminder
that headline index returns do not always tell the full story. The S&P 500 ended the month
slightly lower, but the more muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market
leadership beneath the surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the
Artificial Intelligence (AI) trade, particularly across semiconductors and memory stocks. Yet
pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S. Equities.

Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market, with Value-
oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. While it remains too early to
determine whether the shift will prove durable, October’s rotation may reflect less a retreat
from the AI theme and more a recalibration of valuations and expected returns as market
leadership broadens. For investors, the speed of the shift reinforces the importance of
diversification as the bull market enters a potentially more mature and differentiated phase.

Thought of the Week—Reorienting Around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction
to Capital Goods: AI is reshaping the physical economy, and companies are reorienting
capacity and investment plans to meet surging demand for data centers, power and
infrastructure. Industrial firms are adding or reviving capacity, while data center construction
has increased more than 60% over the past year and is outpacing other major construction
categories. At the same time, robust capital goods orders and long lead times for inputs such

as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers highlight both the strength of demand
and the supply constraints the buildout faces. Taking stock of these signposts, our view
is reinforced that the AI investment cycle remains in its middle innings, creating potential
opportunities for companies supplying the equipment and infrastructure behind the buildout.

2 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Guide to El Niño: What It Is, Why It Matters, and What to Do About It
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
As crude oil flirts with April’s highs, and the price of diesel sits comfortably above $6 per
gallon, disruption to the global energy complex has emerged as a major theme of 2026.
Add yet another variable to the equation: the current “El Niño” event, on track to be one

of the strongest on record. Below we answer frequently asked questions on El Niño,
the potential impact on the global economy and markets and, most importantly, how
investors can position portfolios accordingly.
What is El Niño, and what makes this time different? El Niño is the warm phase
of the “El Niño-Southern Oscillation,” a natural climate pattern occurring every two to
seven years. Simply put, El Niño refers to the warming of sea surface temperatures in

the eastern Pacific, historically leading to drier conditions in some areas (Southeast Asia,
Australia, Central America, southern Africa) and wetter conditions in others (southern
U.S., parts of Latin America) (Exhibit 1).
What makes this episode different? Should forecasts prove correct, this El Niño is on
track to be historic. The National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration’s latest
outlook assigns at least an 83% probability that El Niño’s strength during October-

December will exceed that of any previous episode since 1950. Already, its impact has
been felt via wildfires in Indonesia, the weakest monsoon season in India since 2015,1
and severe flooding from Paraguay to Peru. The upshot: more extreme weather, hotter
temperatures globally, lower crop production in commodity-exporting economies, and
higher prices across major agricultural products.
Exhibit 1: El Niño's Impact Varies Globally.
Geography Typical rainfall pattern Specific impacts

South Asia Weaker summer monsoon risk Lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation
Southeast Asia Drier Lower rice and palm oil production, wildfire risk, food inflation
United States Wetter in the south; warmer/drier in the north Southern flood risk; lower northern heating demand
Central America & Northern South America Drier Drought, lower crop yields, water shortages, food inflation, lower hydropower output
Southern Africa Drier Weaker maize harvests, food inflation, lower hydropower output

Western South America Wetter along the Pacific coast Flooding, infrastructure damage, disrupted fisheries
Southern South America Wetter Flooding and crop damage; potential yield gains in some areas
Australia Drier Drought, agricultural losses, elevated wildfire risk, food inflation
Source: National Oceanic and Atmospheric Administration, World Meteorological Organization, Food and Agriculture Organization, Australian Bureau of Meteorology, World Bank. Data as of

October 30, 2026.
How will El Niño impact the global economy and markets? Which regions will be
most affected? El Niño’s impact on the global economy and markets is likely to be felt
most via commodity price inflation. Alongside higher energy and fertilizer costs resulting
from war in Europe and the Middle East, El Niño could put further upward pressure on
commodities like wheat, sugar, corn, soy, cocoa and coffee as drought (or extreme rain)
weighs on crop yields and production. Latest estimates from the United Nations Food

and Agriculture Organization (FAO) suggest global food prices were up 5.8% year-over-
year (YoY) in September (Exhibit 2). Livestock is also likely to be challenged by higher
temperatures globally, while flooding has already threatened copper production in
Chile. Meanwhile, with hydropower accounting for more than half of the average annual
electricity generation in Brazil, Colombia, Peru and other Latin American countries,2
below-average rainfall in affected areas has huge implications for electricity generation.

As an example, Colombia’s natural gas demand nearly doubled during the 2023-2024 El
Niño event.2 Another key watchpoint will be whether drier conditions reduce water levels
1 India Meteorological Department, Bloomberg. Refers to rainfall between June and September as of September 30, 2026.
2 International Energy Agency, as of October 7, 2026
Investment Implications
We expect El Niño to place upward
pressure on agricultural goods
prices globally. We continue to

see commodities as a strategic
diversifier and hedge against inflation
in portfolios. The U.S. remains our
preferred region with companies in
Industrials and Materials as potential
beneficiaries. Meanwhile, economic
impacts to the emerging world will vary
by country but the broader Emerging
Market (EM) equity market could be
less exposed given its significant tilt
toward Technology.

### Page 3

3 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
in the Panama Canal and other key shipping routes, particularly in light of the Strait of
Hormuz closure.
These pressures won’t be equally distributed and will heavily depend on this El Niño’s
severity. Among the more vulnerable to higher agricultural prices will be heavily import-
dependent countries in the Middle East, Africa, and parts of Asia. So too will economies
that count food as a larger share of consumer spending such as the Philippines (36%),

Indonesia (33%), Egypt (33%), India (28%), and Vietnam (26%) (Exhibit 3). Developed
economies like the U.S. (6%), Canada (10%), and Germany (11%) stand to be better
insulated from a food-related shock to headline inflation.
Within the U.S., some impacts could be positive with a potentially milder winter reducing
heating costs and wetter conditions in the South improving agricultural conditions. A recent
paper from the Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco found that, in addition to putting

upward pressure on personal consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation, strong El Niño
episodes have been historically associated with a temporary boost to employment in the
following year via better construction conditions in the North and weather-event recovery
efforts.3 In California and parts of the Southwest, El Niño will be yet another stress test for
property insurers as flooding and landslide risks increase.
For more vulnerable developing economies, meanwhile, higher food and energy costs could

present a policy dilemma. On one hand, governments in vulnerable areas may move to
introduce fiscal relief to support households and boost growth. History points to plenty of
examples of this, such as Indonesia’s cash assistance in late 2023 or Thailand’s $1.8 billion
in loans to farmers in 2015. On the other hand, elevated inflation expectations and, in
some cases, weaker currencies could limit EM central banks’ room to ease monetary policy,
particularly with several major developed central banks aligned in hiking interest rates. Also

complicating matters: Global sovereign debt surpassed $110 trillion in Q2 2026, leaving
less fiscal space to absorb incoming shocks.4
How can investors position portfolios accordingly? In many ways, the prospect of a
very strong El Niño only reinforces structural trends we’ve identified as defining the coming
years: a return to higher inflation and higher interest rates; elevated commodity prices
amid heightened geopolitical risk and resource/supply shortages; and greater investment in

energy infrastructure and supply chain resilience.
Considering these themes, we continue to like commodities not just as a hedge against
higher inflation but also as a diversification tool in portfolios given their negative correlation
with Equities and bonds. That’s not to mention secular forces placing upward pressure on
commodity prices such as the resource-intensive AI buildout and broader protectionism. To
wit, the Bloomberg Commodity Index, which spans oil, gold, various metals and agricultural

goods has gained nearly 30% this year, handily outperforming the S&P 500 (+1%) and the
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index (-1%).
Within Equities, the U.S. is still our preferred region. While upside risk to inflation remains
a risk to watch going into 2027, food is a relatively small share of the consumer basket in
the U.S., and neither strong services spending nor capital investment should be materially
affected by El Niño. Companies within the Industrials and Materials sectors focused on

precision agriculture, water management and crop protection may be positioned to benefit.
Import-dependent EM economies remain particularly exposed, though impacts will vary
widely depending on the magnitude and timing of weather events. It’s worth noting too that
EM Equities remain heavily concentrated in East Asian markets with substantial exposure to
technology, leaving the broader index arguably less exposed to the economic ramifications
of El Niño than in past episodes. Indeed, even after recent weakness in semiconductor

names, Taiwan, Korea, and China together account for a near-70% of the broader MSCI
Emerging Markets Index.5 We maintain a slight overweight to the region in portfolios given
earnings strength but favor an active approach.

MARKET VIEW
Another Tale of Market Rotation
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
October offered another reminder that headline index returns do not always tell the
full story. The S&P 500 ended the month slightly lower, down almost 1%, but the more
muted headline return masked meaningful shifts in market leadership beneath the
surface. At the center of the volatility was a sharp unwind in the AI trade, particularly

across semiconductors and memory stocks, with the Philadelphia Semiconductor Index
experiencing a 19% intramonth drawdown. The dominant concerns in recent months
have centered on the sustainability of AI infrastructure spending and whether the scale
of investment will ultimately generate sufficient returns. Those only intensified amid a
sharp rise in bond yields after the 10-year U.S. Treasury yield spiked above 5.3% as of
October 5 for the first time since 2002, raising scrutiny over AI financing conditions and

whether higher borrowing costs could increase hurdle rates for continued investment
and slow the pace of capital spending.
Yet the pressure on AI market leaders has not translated into a broad retreat from U.S.
Equities. Instead, capital has moved toward previously lagging areas of the market,
with Value-oriented, defensive and rate-sensitive stocks gaining ground. Value defeated
Growth in October, with the Russell 1000 Value Index outperforming its Growth

counterpart by 10 percentage points, an outcome seen in only about 1% of months since
1979. The move extends Value's outperformance for the year, with the index up 23%
year-to-date compared with down almost 1% for Growth. Participation also improved.
The equal-weighted S&P 500 outperformed its market-cap-weighted counterpart by its
widest monthly gap since 2009 (Exhibit 4A), while the share of S&P 500 constituents
trading above their 200-day moving average rebounded to 62% from just 43% at the

end of September, and 76% of stocks outperformed the index for the month.
Exhibit 4: Market Leadership Has Been Shifting Beneath the Surface.

not possible to invest directly in an index.

The latest rotation is not the first attempt at a change in market leadership this year.
The more important question is what that shift represents: a fundamental reassessment
of the AI trade or another step toward broader, more mature market leadership?
Recent Q3 Technology earnings results have sharpened that debate. Investors are
increasingly looking beyond the scale of AI spending to companies’ ability to translate
that investment into durable growth and attractive returns. The result may be greater

dispersion not only between Growth and Value, but also among AI leaders as investors
become more discerning about which companies can justify the scale of their
investments.
Investment Implications
We continue to look for potential
missing opportunities and favor
diversification across and within asset
classes in a multi-asset portfolio.
In particular, we see potential
opportunities to broaden exposure
beyond AI leaders toward areas
where earnings, capital spending and

productivity trends are improving,
including Value, Small- and Mid-caps,
and select non-U.S. markets, particularly
EMs and Japan. As market leadership
evolves, maintaining diversified
exposures and periodically rebalancing
portfolios can help investors participate
in a broader set of possible return
drivers without becoming overly
dependent on any single theme.

### Page 5

5 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
But greater selectivity does not necessarily signal the end of the AI investment cycle.
YoY growth rates may become harder to maintain after two years of rapid acceleration
as the comparison base rises, but absolute levels of AI capital spending remain elevated.
What matters more is whether spending translates into strong productivity and earnings
growth ahead and whether AI demand continues to support investment. October’s

volatility may therefore reflect less a retreat from the AI theme and more a recalibration
of valuations and expected returns, as other areas of the market begin to contribute
more meaningfully to market performance.
Several developments ahead will help determine whether that rotation has staying
power. One important test is whether broader participation can persist if pressure on AI
stocks continues. Continued strength outside the AI trade would support the case that

investors are reallocating capital rather than broadly reducing risk. The path of monetary
policy will also matter as investors assess where borrowing costs may be headed. The
Federal Reserve’s not-so-hawkish hold in October offered some reassurance, but the
path ahead remains dependent on the evolving balance between inflation and growth.
Any signs of resolution of geopolitical tensions remain an important swing factor
particularly as recent flashpoints in the Middle East have pressured energy prices higher

again. Results of the midterm elections should also provide greater visibility into the
policy landscape for the next few years. While the market does not need every clearing
event to resolve favorably, greater clarity around these uncertainties could help narrow
the range of potential outcomes facing investors.
For investors, October’s sudden change in leadership reinforces the importance of
diversification. Leadership can shift quickly, leaving portfolios that become overly

dependent on a single theme, sector or style particularly vulnerable when it does. We
remain constructive on U.S. Equities and believe that the market may be transitioning
more toward a mid-cycle phase in which returns become broader but also more
measured compared to the sharp acceleration earlier in the cycle. That argues not for
abandoning the market’s long-term winners, but for complementing them with exposure
to additional sources of returns across style, size, sector and region.

### Page 6

6 of 8 November 2, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
Reorienting around AI: From Corporate Plans to Construction to Capital Goods
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
For all the questions (and recent market weakness) hanging over the AI trade, from
monetization and cash burn to financing risk and overcapacity, a measurable aspect is
the buildout itself. Data center construction is lifting construction spending and capital

goods orders, while companies are adding and reviving capacity to meet AI-related
demand. Still, planning delays, access to power, labor scarcity, local politics and the cost
of capital remain meaningful constraints on the buildout’s pace or profitability.
Corporate Plans. Take Caterpillar as one example that has resumed production of a
10-megawatt natural gas turbine discontinued back in 2022. A 175-year old glassmaker,
Corning, is leaning into optical connectivity as AI clusters require faster connections of

optical fiber cabling between servers. Caterpillar and Corning are just two companies
among others pivoting to supply data centers.
Construction to Capital Goods. The AI reorientation is evident in construction
spending as well, where data center construction has outpaced every other major
residential and nonresidential category. Over the last year AI-related construction on data
centers has surged more than 60% while spending on residential, lodging, commercial

or office has declined notably (Exhibit 5A). Data center construction is running at an $85
billion (B) annualized rate this year, versus roughly $49B a year earlier, and now accounts
for 11% of private nonresidential construction, up from just 2% in 2022 according to
the Census Bureau. Similarly, demand for artificial intelligence business equipment and
transportation equipment drove an increase in durable goods orders in September on the
back of new orders for U.S.-manufactured capital goods, suggesting business spending

on equipment remained robust in Q3. Another factor, see Exhibit 5B, where critical
components such as generators, switchboards, chillers and transformers continue to
face long lead times that affect delivery timelines and ultimate profitability for new data
center development.
These signposts reinforce our view that we are only in the middle innings of the AI
investment cycle. From an investment perspective, huge demand for data centers, power
systems, networking infrastructure, specialized chips and other equipment continues

to strain supply, creating both bottlenecks and potential opportunities for companies
powering the buildout.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500/Market-cap Weighted Index is a market-capitalization-weighted index that is widely regarded as the best single gauge of large-cap U.S. equities. The index includes 500 leading

companies and covers approximately 80% of available market capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index includes the same constituents as the capitalization weighted S&P 500, but each company in the S&P 500 EWI is allocated a fixed weight - or 0.2% of the
index total at each quarterly rebalance.
Bloomberg U.S. Aggregate Bond Index is a broad-based, market-capitalization-weighted benchmark that measures the performance of the U.S. investment-grade, U.S. dollar-denominated,

fixed-rate taxable bond market
Bloomberg Commodity Index is a broadly diversified financial benchmark that tracks the price movements of physical commodities through futures contracts.
MSCI Emerging Market Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets (EM) countries.
United Nations Food and Agriculture Food Price Index is a measure of the monthly change in international prices of a basket of food commodities.
Philadelphia Semiconductor Index is a market-cap-weighted index focusing on 30 leading semiconductor firms, offering insights into the tech market.

## Document 299: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-15).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-15
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

## Document 301: CNB Minutes_ Neither hikes nor cuts in the pipeline

Source PDF: all report pdf/CNB Minutes_ Neither hikes nor cuts in the pipeline.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The Bank Board increased interest rates to 4% in a close call,
and we see valid arguments for both positions. Second-round
eﬀects will push inﬂation above the upper bound of the target
corridor for some time, while dwindling economic activity
warrants lots of caution. We see neither hikes nor cuts as the
most likely outcome for quite some time
David Havrlant
The Czech National Bank's decision to raise rates on 5 November was a close
call
Assessing the impact of Hormuz

The Czech National Bank increased the policy rate by 25bp to 4% in
a split vote on 5 November, with Governor Ales Michl and board
members Jan Kubicek, Jan Prochazka and Jakub Seidler voting for

stability. We assess the current situation and the risks to the

depending on whether your concerns are more on the side of

The Bank Board assessed overall risks as pro-inﬂationary. There

have not been signiﬁcantly reﬂected in domestic inﬂation so far.

consumer prices. Deputy Governor Zamrazilova emphasised that
much depends on further developments in the Middle Eastern
conﬂict. Should tensions ease in the near future, a rate hike at the

whether the conﬂict will eventually pass or become the new
normal, with the latter implying persistently high energy costs.
Deputy Governor Frait stated that energy prices remaining at these

levels for such an extended period could have a negative impact on
demand and economic activity, mainly by reducing the real
incomes of households and companies. The current negative
supply shock has hit the Czech economy from a favourable starting
position, but the factors that helped keep inﬂation close to the
target this year are about to fade. The increase in longer-term rates
contributes to the restrictive stance of monetary policy, and implies

some softening in domestic lending activity. The board members
agreed that this is a key factor reducing the need for further
tightening for the time being.
To rise or not to rise?
However, as Jan Kubicek noted, core inﬂation is not slowing down
as previously assumed and is even slightly increasing. This
represents a fundamental concern, especially in combination with
the disappointing real growth performance. That said, producer
prices have embarked on an upward trend and may put further

pressure on the core rate. Jakub Seidler and Karina Kubelkova
emphasised that the renewed price increases in the energy and
food segment are usually the key drivers when it comes to inﬂation

Producer prices will drive consumer prices
Source: CZSO, ING, Macrobond
En gros, among the main reasons for a rate hike in November were:
i) persistent core inﬂation, ii) still robust wage dynamics and iii)
second-round eﬀects expected to bite soon. Among the main

reasons for staying on hold and evaluating were: i) secondary
inﬂationary impacts are so far limited, ii) long-term rates deliver
restriction, iii) disappointing economic activity.
We see the arguments in both directions as valid, which made the
November decision relatively complex and was reﬂected in the
close vote. Time will tell whether the second-round eﬀects that we
believe are just kicking in will prove signiﬁcant without weighing too
heavily on economic activity. Or whether the deteriorating real

purchasing power and dwindling real wage growth could lead to
weaker household consumption, which would in turn put
downward pressure on inﬂation. Both eﬀects are certainly
interconnected, which only underscores the beauty of
macroeconomics, and the formidable power of prices to clear the
market.

### Page 4

Punchy inﬂation across price segments
When it comes to our inﬂation forecast, we deﬁnitely see pro-
inﬂationary pressures when looking ahead. We expect annual
producer price growth to peak at around 7% in industry early next
year and at around 12% in agriculture mid-next year. At the same
time, the annual pace of import prices already quickened to above
8% in September. Those are not low growth rates, and all are going
to feed into consumer prices sooner or later. At the same time, the

koruna continues to weaken against the dollar, making imports
pricier. With all that on board, we see the yearly average of inﬂation
picking up from 2.1% this year to 3.2% next, reaching a peak of just
below 4% in February.

Neither hikes nor cuts in the pipeline
When it comes to core inﬂation, two opposing forces are set for a
duel: second-round eﬀects, coupled with rising import prices,
against a softening housing market and crumbling real purchasing

power. Timing is of the essence. We expect the former to strike ﬁrst,
but the latter to respond without much hesitation. For now, we see
the forces as roughly balanced, keeping average core inﬂation just
below 3% next year.

Consumption could slow tangibly along with

So yes, upbeat inﬂation across price segments warrants restrictive

wage growth will eventually take its toll. Therefore, we see neither
further hikes nor cuts when looking way ahead, with the 4% policy
rate settling in as the new normal.

## Document 311: DeepSeek开放全家桶API：以后可以随心所欲构建APP了

Source PDF: all report pdf/DeepSeek开放全家桶API：以后可以随心所欲构建APP了.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

DeepSeek 正式开放全家桶应⽤  API ：在原有 API 中开启对应参数，即可获得深度思考搜
索、⽂件输⼊等对话功能；语⾳及相关底层能⼒也已开放独⽴接⼝。开发者可以在⾃⼰的
应⽤中轻松实现复杂的对话功能，甚⾄ 1:1 复刻出完整的 DeepSeek App 。
摘要：
在  DeepSeek App （含⽹⻚端）的对话服务中，联⽹搜索、⽂件输⼊、阅读链接、语⾳交互及
安全等能⼒与模型推理相互配合，构成了⽤⼾熟悉的对话体验。⽽开发者如果想要在应⽤中实
现类似功能，除接⼊原有  DeepSeek API 外，往往还需耗费⼤量精⼒去对接外部⼯具、处理调
⽤衔接。
今天，我们正式开放这套全家桶应⽤  API ：在原有  API 中开启对应参数，即可获得深度思考搜
索、⽂件输⼊等对话功能；语⾳及相关底层能⼒也已开放独⽴接⼝（含  OCR 、 ASR 、 TTS
等），⽀持单独或组合调⽤。开发者可以在⾃⼰的应⽤中轻松实现复杂的对话功能，甚⾄  1:1 复
刻出完整的  DeepSeek App 。

全⾯开放应⽤功能，⽀持独⽴调⽤
本次更新将  DeepSeek App 的各项交互功能直接整合进原有  API 。开启对应参数后，相关⼯具
调⽤与上下⽂拼接均由服务端⾃动完成。⽬前， V4.1-Flash 模型（ deepseek-flash ）已在
Messages 、 Responses 与  Chat Completions 接⼝下⽀持以下能⼒：
联⽹搜索：在⾮思考模式下，打开  deepseek_web_search ⼯具后，模型可根据提问⾃动发
起⽹⻚或图⽚搜索并附引⽤作答；
深度思考搜索：在思考模式下，打开  deepseek_web_search ⼯具后，模型可在多步推理中
⾃主联⽹搜索与阅读链接；
⽂件输⼊：⽀持上传  PDF 、 Office 、图⽚等格式的⽂件，完成纯⽂本提取或图⽂混排解析
后，拼⼊对话上下⽂供模型作答；
阅读链接：将  deepseek_read_url ⼯具打开后，模型可⾃动识别⽤⼾消息中的链接，抓取⽹
⻚内容并结合上下⽂作答；
模型安全：开启安全校验参数后，我们将提供基础⻛控，审核模型输⼊输出，命中⻛险时返

回拒答标识。如您对外提供应⽤服务，请务必开启该参数，并承担内容安全审核、⼤模型登
记、 AI 标识等义务。
此外，为⽅便开发者更灵活地接⼊⾃有⼯作流，上述底层能⼒及语⾳能⼒均⽀持作为独⽴接⼝
单独调⽤，包括： Search 、 Fetch 、 OCR 、 ASR 、 TTS 。
使⽤上述接⼝，开发者即可实现与  DeepSeek App 基本⼀致的功能。为直观演⽰这⼀效果，我
们基于新版  API 构建并开源了⼀份轻量的前端  Demo ，⽀持直接导⼊  DeepSeek 历史对话。
Demo 体验⼊⼝： https://cdn.deepseek.com/chat-demo

## Document 319: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 9-13

Source PDF: all report pdf/ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD_ November 9-13.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13
Date:    3 hours ago
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD: November 9-13

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ECONOMIC WEEK AHEAD:
November 9-13
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 7 Nov 2026
View in browser

### Page 2

Photo by Monica Sauro / Unsplash

### Page 3

The S&P 500 fell 2.7% last week, and the Nasdaq dropped 4.9%, both
posting their fourth straight week of losses. Thursday’s 4.4% plunge in the
S&P 500, its worst day since April 2025, came as investors weighed what
the Democratic sweep of Congress means for the AI data center buildout.
Both indexes rebounded Friday on strong jobs data, with the S&P 500 up
0.3% and the Nasdaq up 1.4%.
October’s CPI (Tue) and PPI (Fri) headline this week’s data calendar. Ten

Fed officials are scheduled to speak, and the UK and Eurozone report Q3
GDP. Wednesday’s Veterans Day holiday closes the bond market. Here’s
more:
(1) Earnings season. So far, 88% of S&P 500 companies have reported
their Q3 results. So 60 companies still have to report, and 13 will do so
this week. The "Earnings Squiggles" for 2026, 2027, and 2028 are likely to
rise even further over the remainder of this year (chart). Currently, industry
analysts' consensus is that S&P 500 companies’ aggregate EPS will be

$366.60 this year, $426.30 next year, and $495.10 in 2028. In the past,
Earnings Squiggles tended to fall because analysts were too optimistic.
This time, they haven't been optimistic enough!
(2) Fedspeak. Ten Fed officials are scheduled to speak this week
(Goolsbee, Barkin, Waller, Logan, Schmid, Paulson, Hammack, Daly,
Williams, and Jefferson). These are their first remarks after the
Democratic sweep. These Fed heads are likely hawkish on balance,

especially after Friday's strong employment report.

### Page 4

In early October, the federal funds rate (FFR) futures implied 3.3 rate
hikes over the next 12 months and 1.7 over the next six. Since then, those
projections have fallen to 1.9 and 1.4 (chart). Friday’s employment report
lifted the odds of a December rate hike to 78%. We think the FOMC still
needs another 50bps of increases to reach a neutral FFR.
San Francisco Fed President Mary Daly told CNBC on Friday that “the job
market remains stable and solid, but not booming,” with real wages falling

as pay lags inflation. She expects the Fed “will have to tighten policy
further.”
(3) Inflation. October’s CPI and PPI reports will be released on Tuesday
and Friday, respectively. The Cleveland Fed’s Inflation Nowcasting model
projects that the headline CPI rose 0.24% m/m and the core CPI 0.21%
m/m. That would hold headline CPI inflation at 3.7% y/y and ease core to
2.5% from 2.6% (chart). October would mark the 68th consecutive month
of CPI inflation above 2.0% y/y. The model sees both accelerating in

November, to 3.9% and 2.8% y/y.
The Nowcast also suggests that October’s PCED inflation will remain
firmly above the Fed’s 2.0% target, with the headline and core PCED
inflation rates projected at 3.8% y/y and 3.2% y/y, respectively. Both are
expected to heat up a bit in November.

### Page 5

October's PPI inflation is likely to be hotter than September's headline and
core readings of 6.3% and 5.0% (chart).
One of the more troublesome components of the PPI recently has been
transportation & warehousing services, which jumped 16.0% y/y in
September (chart). Soaring diesel prices are fueling inflation.

### Page 6

(4) ADP & unemployment claims. Payrolls rose 165,000 in October, well
above the 65,000 consensus. The unemployment rate fell to 4.1% from
4.2%. ADP’s weekly employment reading (Tue) follows a four-week
average of 16,750 for the week ended October 10, down from 17,500
(chart). ADP’s monthly report showed private payrolls up 86,000 in
October, beating the 67,000 consensus.
Initial jobless claims (Thu) follow last week's 189,000, with the four-week
average falling to 194,750, the lowest since 1969 (chart). Continuing

claims were 1,718,000 for the week ended October 23, with a four-week
average of 1,709,000.

### Page 7

(5) Global. The UK’s Q3 GDP (Thu) rose at an estimated 0.7% q/q pace,
up from 0.5% in Q2, when real GDP was up 1.4% y/y (chart).
The Eurozone’s updated flash estimate of Q3 GDP (Fri) follows the initial
0.4% q/q reading. Real GDP rose 1.3% y/y in Q3, with Spain leading the
major economies at 2.7% and France down 0.1% (chart).

### Page 8

clause.

## Document 396: Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign Exchange Assets (2026-11-12).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
Three Questions About China’s Hidden Foreign
Exchange Assets
A trillion-dollar gap in foreign exchange purchases raises questions about where
China’s foreign currency assets are held.
Carlos Rawlins/Reuters
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
PUBLISHED
November 11, 2026 8:32 p.m.
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
The PBOC argues—unpersuasively, I think—that it
doesn’t have any hidden foreign exchange reserves. I
would reframe that as hidden foreign currency assets,
as foreign currency lent out (or swapped out) with
domestic financial institutions is a classic way of
masking reserves.
Many in Asia—and some in the IMF—are inclined
to believe the PBOC. Fair enough, it is hard to

challenge an institution as large and powerful (in
some ways) as the PBOC.

### Page 3

3
But to convince, the PBOC really needs to provide
answers to three questions.
1. What explains the current gap between foreign
exchange settlement and the PBOC’s reserves?
Settlement is the purchase of foreign exchange in the
market by the PBOC and the state banks. It
technically implies taking an open foreign currency
position, as the foreign exchange is being bought with
renminbi.
Historically, it correlated with changes in the PBOC’s
balance sheet.

And some gaps before 2012 can be explained. In late
2005 and 2006, the PBOC did over $100 billion in
swaps with the state banks, who used the FX to buy
foreign bonds (portfolio debt outflows surged). Those

### Page 4

4
swaps were unwound after the global financial crisis,
and actually provided some of the foreign currency
that “funded” the China Investment Corporation
when it initially scaled up.
And in 2007 and 2008, the PBOC required that the
state banks hold FX against their domestic RMB
deposits (to keep the FX out of reserves; it was held
on deposit at the PBOC and shows up as “other
foreign assets” on the PBOC’s balance sheet and as
“other, other, short-term” in the balance of payments.*

After 2018, though, there are unexplained gaps
between the FX bought in settlement and the
PBOC’s balance sheet (technically cumulative
monthly changes to capture the fact that FX is held as
its initial CNY purchase price).
There was a net accumulation of FX outside the
PBOC’s balance sheet in 2020 and 2021, when the
CNY was under pressure to appreciate. There are
modest sales in 2023 and 2024 when there is
depreciation pressure. And there are large purchases

in 2025 and the first 9 months of 2026—total
purchases in settlement in the last 12 months (spot)
are $500 to 600 billion, while the increase in the
PBOC’s balance sheet is only around $50 billion over
the same period.

### Page 5

5
The gap over time is now $1 trillion. The PBOC
needs to be able to answer a simple question: who
holds this FX, and who holds the associated FX
market risk (given the FX has been purchased with
RMB).
The monthly pattern of growth in settlement strongly
suggests it is backdoor intervention; there are sales
when spot is weak (economically) relative to the fix,
and purchases when spot is either at the fix or just
stronger than the fix (the strong edge of the band

hasn’t been used). The pattern of purchases and sales
thus looks exactly like that of a central bank
maintaining a tight band around a (crawling) peg.
If the PBOC cannot credibly explain this gap, there
isn’t much more to discuss. The PBOC argues that it
hasn’t swapped FX with the state banks or put foreign
currency on deposit at the state banks. Fair enough.

### Page 6

6
But someone is buying in settlement; the PBOC
ought to know who…
2. Missing interest income
There is an obvious tell here—Bloomberg reported
back in 2022 that the PBOC transferred $158 billion
in accumulated interest income to the Ministry of
Finance as part of a special dividend to hold the
budget deficit down. Again, fair enough. But
absolutely nothing changed in the FX reserves held
on the PBOC’s balance sheet or in the balance of
payments.
That highlights a broader issue. Since the end of

2016, the PBOC’s balance sheet FX hasn’t really
changed.

### Page 7

7
Peer institutions generally have an upward slope to
their reserves as a result of “gains from reserve
management” or “reinvestment of interest income.” **
The Central Bank of China (Taipei) and Japan’s
Ministry of Finance are good examples.
The interest on ~$3 trillion in reserves should be
around $100 billion a year. A bit less during the “low-
for-long” era before the pandemic, when SAFE wasn’t
getting much income out of its euro portfolio. A bit

more now, when U.S. short-term rates are well above 3
percent and SAFE’s legacy agencies carry yields of 5
or 6 percent. $100 billion over 10 years is real money
—roughly a trillion dollars.
If SAFE has steadily been selling its interest income,
it should say so (the fact that BOP reserves have often
diverged from the PBOC balance sheet suggests a
more complex story).

### Page 8

8
Moreover, if SAFE has been transferring the FX to
one of China’s many sovereign wealth funds
(including those controlled by SAFE) or the policy
banks, it should say so.
But SAFE cannot say what you see is what we really
have if it cannot explain how it handles the interest
on its now $3.5 trillion in reserves.
3. The accounting for the PBOC’s entrusted loans
and other foreign currency claims on domestic
financial institutions, notably the policy banks

FX handed over to a sovereign wealth fund to manage
doesn’t appear in reserves. It isn’t a liquid reserve asset.
But it should appear elsewhere in the balance of
payments as either portfolio debt or portfolio equity.
And FX handed over to the policy banks through
mechanisms such as entrusted loans (or a simple
deposit or swap) doesn’t count as a foreign reserve—
the foreign currency claim is on a domestic financial
institution, and that isn’t considered a “foreign” asset.

There is no doubt that the PBOC has domestic
claims on China’s financial institutions. The use of
“entrusted” loans to fund the policy banks in 2010
was reported by Caixin and the Financial Times.

### Page 9

9
$95 billion in entrusted loans were converted into
equity in the CDB and China Exim back in 2015
(giving SAFE majority equity stakes in both
institutions through its Buttonwood investment
vehicle).
And the SAFE’s 2020 annual report contained a ten-
year progress report on the “diversified use” of China’s
FX (basically, the non-reserve use) which indicated
that SAFE continued to provide entrusted loans to
small, medium, and large Chinese financial

institutions.
Now perhaps these instruments have been unwound
—the China Development Corporation (per Stephen
Green, a friend) has reduced its foreign currency
balance sheet over the last few years. But that should
lead the foreign currency to flow back to SAFE
absent another use—and that increase in FX isn’t
showing up in the PBOC’s reserves.
In other words, SAFE should provide a transparent
accounting of the FX that has been bought but
transferred to other financial institutions and any

ongoing foreign currency exposure to the domestic
banking system (including the policy banks).
These aren’t big asks. The settlement gap is big and
obvious, interest income is a known issue, and SAFE
itself has reported its use of entrusted loans in the
past. There is no issue around the past use of swaps

### Page 10

10
either. A substantial share of the banks’ foreign
currency assets are funded domestically. It should be
possible for SAFE to explain the role, if any, it has
played in this funding.
There is, of course, another issue around China’s
balance of payments: the growth in foreign currency
external assets funded out of domestic foreign
currency deposits.
China argues that the growing foreign assets of its
state banks are simply a natural part of a growing

domestic financial system. But there is more to the
story.
Onshore FX deposits have tended to rise when
economics say they should not, and fall when the
economics say they should (offshore FX deposits
behave as expected). In 2020 and 2021, FX deposits

### Page 11

11
rose even when there was more yield on RMB
deposits.
And RMB deposits have continued to rise in 2025
and 2026 even with the RMB’s appreciation
offsetting the higher dollar deposit rate. This isn’t an
accident: banks just raised the interest rate that they
pay on dollar deposits to encourage exporters NOT to
convert their export proceeds into RMB and thus
take pressure off the PBOC (per Reuters).
In other words, the pattern of the banks’ foreign

currency asset growth suggests that they are offsetting
appreciation pressure by bidding for FX (or receiving
it from other state actors), not that FX flows into the
banks during periods when onshore residents worry
about depreciation.

### Page 12

12
Right now, the “settlement gap” is the most pressing
issue. FX deposit growth in excess of FX loan growth
can explain FX outflows of around $150 billion in the
four quarters to Q3. The FX outflows implied by
settlement reached $600 billion over the last four
quarters. Together they account for the bulk of
China’s reported $825 billion plus (I estimate a Q4
2025 to Q3 2026 surplus of around $900 billion) in
outflows implied by the reported current account.***

### Page 13

13
It goes without saying that this matters because the
weak RMB is the central reason for China’s massive
export outperformance since 2023—and that export
outperformance has continued in 2026.****
* I have been doing this a long time; I actually do
know most of the historical quirks in China’s balance

### Page 14

14
of payments data.
** The MoF can count a portion of its unrepatriated
gains from accumulated interest on its large reserves
against the budget deficit, but since the actual
interest is held abroad, it still needs to borrow
domestically against those reserves (domestic JGB
issuance and foreign reserves both go up).
*** My estimate of a larger “true” current account
comes from moving gold imports over to the financial
account, and from an assumption that undercounting

of the reinvested earnings of funds held offshore
explains the obvious problem in the investment
income account.
**** The spike in imports in Q1 wasn’t entirely real.
China was slow to adjust its import price index for
the rise in semiconductor/chip prices. This inflated
both import and export volumes in Q1. The rise in
gold imports this year also registers in the import
volume series—though the rise is best understood as
“importing” a financial asset.

### Page 15

15
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

中國兩年期殖利率 1.25% 中國10月官方製造與服務業景氣再度下行，預期接下來有降準50bp空間。
新興市場股 中立 回測顯示美期中選後新亞反彈機會大，然恐適逢美伊戰事再度開打的風險， 留意波動震盪，新亞相對看好台灣和印度，拉美因巴西選舉逆轉轉中立。
新興亞洲 中立 新興亞洲中相對看好台灣與印度，台股憑藉完整供應鏈優勢與穩健的獲利 上修趨勢，仍具相對吸引力，印度有評價面保護優勢。

中國A 中立 聚焦AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技族群(但短期波動高)、稀缺 原物料及高附加出口價值等產業，搭配高股息金融股平衡投資組合風險。
香港 中立 削價競爭雖告尾聲但內需乏力影響獲利，並關注資本支出轉為獲利進程。
台灣 正向 留意美期中選後反彈後將面臨美伊戰事波動，惟長線看好台股投資願景。

資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
重點摘要
3
• 五 中 全 會 公 報 隱 含 官 方
對 完 成 年 度 經 濟 目 標 具
相 當 信 心 ， 短 期 推 出 大
規 模 景 氣 刺 激 政 策 可 能
性降低。
• 五 中 全 會 前 後 顯 示 本 輪
政 治 整 頓 力 度 短 期 有 所
降溫， 但 整 體 水 準 仍 明
顯高於歷史均值。
《投資關鍵-反腐短暫趨緩，關注4Q景氣能否達標》
中國第三季實質GDP年增4.5%，前三季累計年增
4.6%，位居官方全年4.5%至5.0%目標區間下緣，
整體延續外強內弱格局。出口強勁，加上政策刺激
提供經濟底部支撐，然投資與消費兩大內需引擎同
步降溫，內生需求不足的結構性問題未有效改善。
展望後市，官方將擴大超長期特別國債與地方政府

專項債發行規模，加快重大基建項目落地，並持續
透過房地產去庫存、城中村改造等定向措施穩定內
需，搭配結構性寬鬆政策引導融資成本下行。惟五
中全會公報強調「確保完成全年經濟社會發展主要
目標任務」來看，官方對完成年度成長目標具相當
信心，因此第四季GDP年增率僅需維持約4.5%至
4.6%，短期推出大規模景氣刺激政策可能性降低。
本次五中全會主要聚焦黨的治理體系、監督機制與
自我革命等議題，並未釋出新一輪大規模財政或貨
幣刺激訊號。此外，觀察高盛編製的反腐敗代理指
標，五中全會前後雖略有回落，顯示本輪政治整頓
力度短期有所降溫，但整體水準仍明顯高於歷史均
值。隨著2027年新一屆黨領導層改組逐步接近，反
腐與監管力道仍有再度升溫的可能。在此背景下，
地方官員風險偏好與投資意願恐持續受到抑制，進
而削弱政策寬鬆措施向實體經濟的傳導效果，使中
國經濟仍面臨政策支持與內生需求不足並存的挑戰。
留意反貪腐力道若再度上升將壓抑政策傳導效果
資料來源：高盛，2026/10/30。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/11/2。

《投資關鍵-回測顯示美期中選後短線有反彈契機》
美國11/3將舉行參眾兩院的國會選舉，除視為對
當任總統的支持度檢驗之一，參眾兩院的黨派席次
也可能影響接下來政策推行速度。
回顧過去美股期中選舉期間，新興市場選前大多表
現壓抑，惟選後大多繳出正報酬佳績，上漲比率超
越五成。若以參眾兩院控制權皆翻轉的兩個年度
1994年、2006年來看，1944年因美聯準會啟動快
速升息循環，加上墨西哥金融危機爆發，導致選後
新興市場進入近一年的空頭走勢。然2006年即便
也是分裂政府，全球宏觀環境正受惠全球貿易繁榮、
商品超級周期推動經濟成長，新興市場選後12個

月累積報酬相當亮眼。
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。
歷年美股期中選舉期間，新興市場表現先抑後揚

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投

重點摘要
5
• 同樣面臨美國政治版圖翻
轉，新興市場指數表現卻
顯著差異，意味選後更取
決於景氣與獲利週期，而
非國會席次變化本身。
• 過去美股期中選舉前。台
股的平均表現最為壓抑，
但選後反彈幅度亦最為顯
著，且正報酬機率維持在
較高水準。
比較1994年與2006年同樣面臨美國政治版圖翻轉，
指數表現卻顯著差異，意味選後更取決於景氣與獲
利週期，而非國會席次變化本身。
進一步檢視1990年以來美國期中選舉的各市場表
現，中國上證指數受自身政策與景氣循環影響較深，
與美國期中選舉關聯性相對有限外，多數市場皆呈
現選前承壓、選後回升的特徵。其中，台股於期中
選舉前的平均表現最為壓抑，但選後反彈幅度亦最
為顯著，且正報酬機率維持在較高水準。或反應美

國政治不確定性於選後下降，市場焦點重新回歸景
氣與企業獲利基本面，帶動資金自美國逐步向Beta
值較高的市場擴散。進一步而言，若相關國家具備
較穩健的經濟基本面、企業獲利動能或產業成長題
材支撐，則更有機會在風險偏好回升與資金重新配
置的過程中，成為資金優先流入的受惠對象
資料來源：Bloomberg，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/28。
